Everywhere else in the game — the Classic header row (`HeaderTickspeedRowClassic`) and the Modern AD‑tab `TickspeedRow` — gates the tickspeed display on `Tickspeed.isUnlocked || EternityChallenge(9).isRunning`. Now both themes show identical content at every progress point: nothing extra before unlock, tickspeed info after.\n117\t\n118\t## Why not the other direction\n119\t\n120\tThe Classic row carries the real **Tickspeed buy / Buy Max buttons**; the Modern row has none. complete.\n\n## What was wrong\n\nBoth screenshots are the header center region of a freshly started save. The Modern theme showed an extra tickspeed block:\n\n```\nAD Tickspeed: 1.000 / sec\n×1.125 faster per upgrade\n```\n\n…that the Classic theme did not. On a fresh save, tickspeed isn't unlocked yet (`Tickspeed.isUnlocked` requires the 2nd Antimatter Dimension to have been bought). Everywhere else in the game — the Classic header row (`HeaderTickspeedRowClassic`) and the Modern AD‑tab `TickspeedRow` — gates the tickspeed display on `Tickspeed.isUnlocked || EternityChallenge(9).isRunning`. Only `HeaderTickspeedRowModern` rendered it unconditionally, so it leaked pre‑unlock info that Classic (correctly) hid.\n\n## The fix\n\nAdded the same visibility gate to `HeaderTickspeedRowModern.vue`: it now computes `isVisible` in `update()` and only renders the two tickspeed lines when tickspeed is actually unlocked. `GameSpeedDisplay` stays rendered — it already self‑gates and is empty on a fresh save — so nothing else changes. Now both themes show identical content at every progress point: nothing extra before unlock, tickspeed info after.\n\n## Why not the other direction\n\nThe Classic row carries the real **Tickspeed buy / Buy Max buttons**; the Modern row has none. So \"make them agree\" could *not* mean collapsing both onto the button‑less Modern component or dropping Classic's gate — that would strip or alter a control Classic players can currently reach.
